<!-- page 1 -->
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, October 13, 2015 
 
PRESENT: Robert K. Black Jr., Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jason E. Whiting, Vice-Chairman; and 
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman were present. Jesse Thompson, Supervisor was absent. 
9:11 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation led by Supervisor Whiting.  
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Terry Hill, Commander of VFW Post 9907 in Show Low, addressed the Board and 
asked the Board  to recognize the v eteran employees that work for the county. He reported that the VFW Post 
9907 and the Elks Lodge  2090 will be hosting a Veteran’s Day dinner, for veterans at the Elks Lodge in Show 
Low. Commander Hill reported on a military heritage book project by the Show Low High School students, which 
will include information from interviews of local veterans, regarding their military service. 
  
      CONSENT AGENDA: a) Minutes: September 27, 2015 b) Warrant & Voucher Report over $1000 September 
2015 c) Constable Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1, August; Winslow Precinct #2, July & September; and Show 
Low Precinct #5, September 2015 d) Changes to the Board of Supervisors meeting schedule for December 2015 
e) Name an unnamed existing road in the Pinedale/Claysprings area to “Mesquite Lane” in Section 17, Township 
10 North, Range 20 East f) Memorandum of Understanding allowing the BIA, Navajo Nation and Navajo -Hopi 
Land Commission Office, and Navajo County to work in cooperation and partnership on Transportation projects 
within Navajo County g) New 15MPH speed limit sign installations on Chevelon Road h) Brownsfield Coalition 
Intergovernmental Agreement extension with th e City of Flagstaff i) Amendment to the Intergovernmental 
Agreement between the Apache County School Superintendent, the Apache County Superior Court, the Navajo 
County School Superintendent and the Navajo County Superior Court; to define the educational r esponsibilities 
stemming from the closure of the Apache County Juvenile Detention Facility j) CDBG Application forms E -3.1 
and E-3 k) Appointment to the Navajo County Democratic Committee as a Precinct Committeeman to fill an 
existing vacancy in precinct 5 9.01: Marian Gail McCollister l) Letter to Navajo County Citizens for the FY16 
Budget Book: Supervisor Williams made a motion to authorize the Chairwoman to sign the items in the consent 
agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
  
      NAVAJO COUNTY LIBRARY DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session: CONSENT AGENDA:  
Supervisor Williams made a motion  to enter into the Navajo County Library District Board of Directors  
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      Contract between Navajo County Library District and the Yavapai County School Superintendent for E -rate 
Consultation services to obtain E -rate discounts for the Library District and the fourteen member libraries : 
Supervisor Will iams made a motion  to approve the consent a genda as presented ; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter into 
regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS:  
a. Recognition of Robert Yellowhair for his artwork and his life : Supervisor Whiting recognized Robert 
Yellowhair for the legacy of his artwork  which includes drawings, paintings, saddles and belt buckles . 
Mr. Yellowhair introduced himself as having a Zuni bloodline and spoke of his artistic life. He indicated 
that he tells stories through his artwork of different tribes, which also include the Navajo, Hopi and Apache 
tribes.

<!-- page 2 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 10-13-2015 
    Page 2 of 4 
 
 
b. BOARD OF SUPERVISORS ACTING AS THE BOARD OF EQUALIZATION:  Supervisor 
Williams made a motion to enter into the Board of Equalization session; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
Consideration and possible approval of the Board of Equalization Hearing Officer’s recommendation on 
hearings conducted on September 23, 2015 for Petition for Review  of Real Property Valuation: Melissa 
Buckley, Clerk of the Board presented the item. Mrs. Buckley re cognized Amber Ferguson, Senior 
Secretary for her work on the Board of Equalization process this year.  Supervisor Whiting made a 
motion to approve the Board of Equalization Hearing Officer’s recommendation on hearings conducted 
on September 23, 2015 for Petition for Review of Real Property Valuation ; motion seconded by  
Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Supervisor Whiting made a motion  to 
return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
HUMAN RESOURCES: Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Eric Scott reviewed the 18 
item personnel action report . Supervisor Whiting made a motion  to approve the Personnel Action Report as 
presented; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session: Supervisor 
Williams made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
a. CONSENT AGENDA:  
i) Amendment #2 of Contract #ADHS14 -053056 with Arizona Department of Health Services for the 
WIC Program for October 1, 2015 to September 30, 2016 in the amount of $360,795.00: 
ii) Memorandum of Agreement with Chinle Unified School District and Southwest Food Excellence from 
October 1, 2015 to September 30, 2018: 
iii) Memorandum of Agreement with St. Michael’s Association for Special Education from October 1, 
2015 to September 30, 2018: 
iv) Memorandum of Agreement with Heber/Overgaard Unified School District from October 1, 2015 to 
September 30, 2018: 
v) Memorandum of Agreement with Seba Dalkai Boarding School from October 1, 2015 to September 
30, 2018: 
Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve the consent agenda as presented ; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of a Memorandum of Understanding with Little Colorado Medical 
Center for a closed Point of Dispensing site: Mary Herring, Health Director presented the item. 
Supervisor Black made a motion to approve the Memorandum of Understanding with Little Colorado 
Medical Center for a closed Point of Dispensing site; motion seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote 
unanimous approving the motion. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to return to regular sessi on; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
RECORDER:  
a. Consideration and possible approval of a contract with ES&S for purchase of Election Equipment: Laura 
Sanchez, Recorder presented the item and answered questions of the Board. Supervisor Williams made 
a motion to approve the contract with ES&S for purchase of E lection Equipment; motion seconded by 
Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of Super Precincts : Laura Sanchez, Recorder presented the item . 
Supervisor Whiting and Chairwoman Whitesinger thanked Mrs. Sanchez for her work and leadership.

<!-- page 3 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 10-13-2015 
    Page 3 of 4 
 
 
James Jayne stated that Navajo County takes  reliable and credible  elections very seriously. Mr. Jayne  
spoke about the supe r precincts and  indicated that this will  create an easier and more effective way  of 
voting and access for the voters throughout the county. Mr. Jayne thanked Mrs. Sanchez for her leadership 
on this project. Supervisor Williams made a motion to approve the Super Precincts; motion seconded 
by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
ADMINISTRATION: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution Number 39-15, urging the Arizona 
Senate Delegation and the Senate to support the Diné College Act of 2015: Melissa Buckley, Clerk of the Board 
discussed the Dine College Act of 2015. Supervisor Williams made a motion to adopt Resolution Number 39-
15, urging the Arizona Senate Delegation and the Senate to support the Diné College Act of 2015 ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      FINANCE: Consideration and possible approval of the sale of surplus County property by public online auction 
to be held in November 2015 : Joyce Alexander discussed the item.  Supervisor Williams made a motion  to 
approve the sale of surplus County property by public online auction to be held in November 2015 ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Whiting. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
      NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT Board of Directors Session: CONSENT AGENDA: 
Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Flood Control District Board of Directors 
Session; motion seconded by Supervisor Black . Vote  unanimous approving the motion. Community Rating 
System Recertification letter: Supervisor Whiting made a motion to approve the consent agenda as presented; 
motion seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Supervisor Williams made a 
motion to enter into regular session ; motion seconded by  Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the 
motion.   
      COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Black reported he attended meetings with the Public Works department regarding flood control issues 
on the roads in District I . He stated that he , Supervisor Thompson and Laura Sanchez, Recorder represented 
Navajo County at various parades. He indicated he will be attending the Low Mountain Chapter Veteran’s Day 
parade a nd the parade at the Tuba City f air. Supervisor Black expressed appreciation to everyone for their 
assistance with the various parades and gave a special thanks to Alberto Peshlakai for his participation. He spoke 
of his partnership with the Navajo Nation sta ff and their discussions regarding affordable housing. He indicated 
that he attended  discussions with the Office of the Navajo Nation Vice-President for health initiatives , which 
included creating bike and walking trails. Supervisor Williams reported on the Arizona Supreme Court Justices 
visit to Navajo County at Show Low High School. Supervisor Williams indicated he will attend the CSA Summit 
and stated that many issues will be discussed at the Summit regarding the counties . Supervisor Whiting spoke 
about the Bread of Life Mission ’s fundraiser. He expressed thanks to  all those that attended  and to those that 
supported the event  to raise money for the Bread of Life Mission. He reported on the EPA meeting and the 
discussions held to help support the APS Cholla Plant and their employees, which is vital for the  people and the 
economy in our region. Supervisor Whiting talked about attending the Healthy Forest Conference  which was 
hosted by SRP, which addressed the subject of  current forest restoration. He indicat ed there was representation 
from the Governor’s office, Senator Flake’s and Senator McCain’s office and the industry services, including 
Navajo County at the meeting . Supervisor Whiting expressed his appreciation to Brandt Clark, Attorney and 
Melissa Buckl ey and team regarding their work with  the White Mountain Lakes Recreation  Improvement 
District’s Advisory Board. James Jayne recognized Mary Springer and Catrina Jenkins for the October newsletter 
submitted by Emergency Management. He thanked Mary Springer for the incredible job she is doing, in working

<!-- page 4 -->
Board of Supervisor Minutes 10-13-2015 
    Page 4 of 4 
 
 
with partners and stakeholders in our County, in furthering the Emergency Management services  and 
preparedness in Navajo County. Brandt Clark, Attorney spoke about the beauty of the White Moun tains in the 
White Mountain Apache Tribe area in the fall. Mr. Clark indicated he also visited the areas of  the Wallow Fire 
and the destruction which occurred there. He stated the importance of preserving the forests. Mr. Clark reported 
on the  Arizona Supreme Cou rt Justices visit to Navajo County . Mr. Clark expressed his appreciation of his 
supervisor, Brad Carlyon.  He reported on the Active Shooter T raining exercises that will be presented  by the 
Navajo County Emergency Management team, at the county complex . Chairwoman Whitesinger spoke about 
the Health District’s classes regarding the MAPP process, and their discussion of increasing adequate pre-natal 
services. She indicated that Summit Healthcare and the Indian Health Services were part of the discussions with 
Navajo County. Chairwoman Whitesinger thanked Nick Lund for his leadership with the TRACKS programs 
and their partnership with the Town of Pinetop -Lakeside and the Sitgreaves area. Chairwoman Whitesinger 
indicated she welcomed the participants to the Annual Bike Tour in the White Mountains, with Mayor Smith and 
Pinetop-Lakeside Town Manager, Evelyn Racette. Chairwoman Whitesinger thanked the CARE Committee for 
the Book Drive and to all of the employees who donated children’s books for the young children of Pinetop -
Lakeside. She spoke of the  importance of the  Arizona Supreme Court  Justices visit t o Navajo County.  
Chairwoman Whitesinger thanked Mary Springer for the Emergency Management report at the White Mountain 
Regional Transportation Committee’s meeting. Chairwoman Whitesinger reported on the youth pass giveaway 
at Sunrise Park Resort last weekend. She stated the first day of anticipated snow making at Sunrise Park will be 
on October 26th.  
      ADJOURN: At 10:38 a.m. Supervisor Williams made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
APPROVED:       DATED: October 27, 2015 
 
Dawnafe Whitesinger, Chairwoman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors 
 
 
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
Melissa W. Buckley, Clerk 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
